    - *Left: Rust's float-to-integer casts saturate. x86-64 at its baseline (SSE2) has no instruction that does, so the compiler converts one lane at a time, about a tenth of the frame here.   - *An error is placed by whole characters. A letter typed where none goes, `é` or `🇺`, was an error one byte wide, inside the letter, and placing it in the page panicked.   - *Data far past 10^22 ticks as any other. The tick step's power of ten came from a table that stops at 10^22, so a value of 1e300 asked for 10^277 ticks. Past the table, the power is the literal as Rust reads it, correctly rounded on every platform, and a range no step divides has no ticks (`ticks_hold_at_the_ends_of_f64`).*
  - *A JSON data row lines up with its columns by name. A row that named its columns in another order put its values under the wrong ones, and a row that left one out panicked validation.   *(Done: SPEC §7.1, §7.2, §8.2, §9.2, ADR-0013.*
  - *A version is the deck as it was just after one change the history keeps, with the data files its sources named then (`DeckDoc::at`, a fork at the change's last operation). Loro stores one large commit as several changes that continue each other, with the same author, message, and time: `DeckDoc::changes` joins them, so a version is a whole commit, named by its first operation's id.*
  - *`scaena history` lists the versions; `--at` gives one's deck (`--scn` as source); `--diff A[,B]` says what changed from one to another or to the deck now, state by state (each state's own fields, and its nodes resolved with the overrides, as `diff` says them), then the deck's own fields, the states' order, and the data files whose bytes differ; `--restore` makes one the deck again with its data files, one change (`history --restore <id>`), checked and refused as a patch is (`scaena_ops::history`). `deck_history` is the MCP tool (15 tools); the assistant has none, since its module keeps no CRDT.*
  - *The editor's Versions tab reads the history with the history's own module (`changes`, `at`), loaded the first time. A version chosen is drawn read only by a session of its own (`Player.viewVersion`, `versionPng`), or says why it cannot be (a file it names taken out since), beside what changed from it to the deck now or to another version (`Player.compareVersions`). Restore (`Player.restoreVersion`) writes as any operation's edit does (`Session::write`): kept for the save, the data file's bytes before kept as the bundle's own, so the save records the restore and nothing as changed outside Scaena. The source takes the deck as one CodeMirror change that carries the files written, before and after; its undo writes them back and its redo writes them again (`Player.writeFiles`).*

  *(Done: ADR-0016, SPEC §3.6, §7.1, §7.2, §8.1, §8.2, §9.2, ADR-0013.*
  - *A theme edit is RFC 6902 operations on the theme the deck names, checked as a re-theme is: refused where the deck would not validate in the theme it leaves (a name it uses taken out, E102; a value the schema refuses, E106), and otherwise written in canonical form, as `save` writes it, with the lint delta.   - *The history keeps the theme the deck names beside its data files (`scaena_store::kept_paths`): an edit is one change with the theme's bytes (`theme_edit: tokens/color/paper`), a save records the theme as it writes it, and a theme changed on disk goes in as a change by `fs`, once for a history from before. A version is the deck, its data files, and its theme; comparing one held before the history kept the theme says nothing of it (`differing` skips bytes a side does not know).*

Each change is one `theme_edit` by `user` (`Player.themeEdit`). W427 finds a figure a file changed behind the deck's back, and the claim with it, each with its fix (`tests/lint/W427/`). A quote's source the deck lacks is E102; its row, column, or empty cell E103; its format or transform E106. `style_text` takes `quote`; a figure is styled whole, typing into it makes it words, and typing beside it goes in a run of its own. In the editor, the Data tab's Quote quotes the cell focused in the characters selected, its row by the columns of text that tell it apart (`web/quotes.mjs`, 10 checks). The site's pages register it (`web/src/offline.ts`, only where `VITE_BUNDLE` says this is the site). It keeps every file as it installs, so the engine and each module loaded later (the subsetter, the history, the PDF writer, what the assistant reads, the hyphenation patterns, the themes) come from the cache. It answers a deck's files from the network and keeps them for when there is none, and lets other origins and `scaena serve`'s events pass. Its cache is named for the files' contents, so a new build takes over once no page uses the last. The pages say "Works offline". `web/offline.mjs` opens the site once, takes the network away, then plays every state of the demo deck, edits and lints it, and downloads it (15 checks). The editor's module does not change; the site gains `sw.js`, `manifest.webmanifest`, and `icon.svg`.*

Each tick keeps its key, the date written whole, so transitions and gridlines match as they did. `a_time_axis_ticks_on_calendar_boundaries` holds a year's quarters (`Jan 2024`, `Apr`, `Jul`, `Oct`) and a new year's crossing (`Sep 2025` … `Jan 2026`, `Feb`); no golden draws a time axis.
- [x] 2.82 A contrast error offers a fix: the theme's color that reads against what lies behind the text (Jay, 2026-10-07: as the 2.77 walk recommended).
    - Done. A text node's E110 or E111 carries the patch that chooses it, as the inspector chooses a color, the theme's color whose worst reading is best: a `choose` of `style/color` at the state it reads worst in, written where its color lives (`scaena_engine::lint::contrast::recolor`, after every format is linted). Lint keeps it only where the text reads in it in every state and format the choice reaches. Text is taken away from what lies behind any text, so the pixels painted once judge each color. First, each color is screened over what lies under the runs that failed, as lint's own reading kept it, without sorting (`reads`, held to `read`'s verdict by `reads_is_read_at_the_bar`): only a color that reads there is judged where the choice reaches, which lays states out and paints them. The bench gate caught the version without the screen at +106% on `lint_layout/b4`, the torture deck, whose `case` over the mesh reads in no color; with it, lint on the torture deck takes what it did, within the noise of five runs each. A probe color the text has nowhere tells the runs that take the node's color from those `style_text` gave their own. `scaena_core::patch::compile_alone` compiles the choice without setting quoted figures again, so a fix changes no text. The trails deck re-themed in Daybreak sets its storm slide's title and note dark on the dark photo (E111 at 1.09:1, E110 at 1.07:1); each fix sets `surface`, and `lint --fix` leaves no contrast error. The torture deck's `case` over the mesh gets none: no color of the theme reads over the whole gradient, at rest and at the end of its hold. A chart's text and a table's are set in the theme's roles for them, and get no fix. `a_contrast_fix_chooses_the_color_that_reads_wherever_the_choice_reaches` holds the fixture's fix, its `lint --fix`, no fix where the choice reaches a light panel and the dark surface both, and one written in a state's own delta where the color lives there. The editor's mark says an object set whole by its keys (`color: surface`), and `web/edit-deck.mjs` takes each fix on the storm slide in Daybreak in one click, sees each error go, and undoes both. The editor's engine module is 2,867.93 kB gzipped, as Vite reports it: 9.4 kB more than after 2.79, the most any Phase 2 task has added, which leaves 132 kB under SPEC §15's 3 MB.

- [x] 2.84 The demo deck in 9:16: trails lists `9:16` and lints clean there, its 16:9 as it was.
    - Done. Trails could not be laid out well in 9:16 by slots alone. Its process slide is a grid of four steps across, and a container's tracks were the same in every format. So a node may now say how it lays out in each of the deck's formats (ADR-0020, deck format 0.13, SPEC §3.4):
      - `formats: {"9:16": {…}}` sets its `at`, `size`, `align`, and `transform` there, a container's `axis`, `gap`, `distribute`, `padding`, `cols`, `rows`, and `areas`, and a text's `maxLines`;
      - `scaena_engine::project` puts each in place of the node's own when it lays the deck out in that format;
      - validation finds a format the deck does not lay out in, or a cell past its grid (E102), and what the type does not take, or `formats` in a delta or the overrides (E106);
      - a node placed anew in each format is not W302.

      In 9:16, trails stacks its process steps with an arrow down between each, puts the change slide's before over its after, and gives the budget table the full width. Its titles keep their cells, and its quote takes a fourth line.

      Its plan line's label said "Plan: 6.7 miles a month". In 9:16 the bars stand too close for that to sit clear of them (E111), so it says "Plan: 6.7" in both formats: the caption gives the units. A rule's label now also moves along the rule, past the marks that rise through it, where its start is not clear (`charts::compile`).

      Tests: `crates/scaena-core/tests/validate.rs`, `crates/scaena-engine/tests/formats.rs` and `charts.rs`, the W302 clean fixture, and the edit-deck walk, which shows the deck in 9:16.

      The editor writes only a node's own layout: a drag in 9:16 moves it in 16:9 too. Writing the format's layout where a node has one is a follow-up.

- [x] 2.85 The editor places a node in the format shown: where the node lays out anew there, a drag, a resize, or an arrow key moves it there alone.
    - Done (ADR-0020). `place` takes `format` and `anew` (SPEC §7.3):
      - with `format`, a node that has its own layout there (`formats`) is placed in that layout's `at`, and its own `at` stays, so the deck's own canvas is as it was;
      - a node with none is placed as before, in its own `at`, in every format;
      - `anew` gives it a layout of its own there, placed so; refused in a format the deck does not lay out anew, and `fork` is refused with `format` where the layout is the node's own.
    - The engine's targets carry the format they were asked in (`Targets.format`), so every gesture's `place` ops and `inspect --snap`'s say it, and the editor's patches with them.
    - The editor's menus offer "Place anew in this format" for a node with no layout of its own in the format shown; it copies the node's placement there.
    - Tests: `crates/scaena-core/tests/patch.rs`, the WASM session's drag in 9:16 (`a_drag_in_a_format_moves_its_node_there_alone`), and the edit-deck walk, which moves the trails title in 9:16 and finds it where it stood in 16:9, and places the budget's note anew in 9:16.

    - Done (ADR-0020). Probing what 2.84 and 2.85 left beside them found two faults:

Now `Session::duplicating` places it in each format the node lays out in: the one shown, the deck's own canvas, and each with a layout of its own there, each clear of the rest where there is room. In trails, `budget-why` duplicated in 9:16 went beside itself there and onto itself in 16:9; now it stands clear in both.
      - **A paste kept the placements the clip had in its formats.** Pasted into its own deck in 9:16, the copy landed on the node it came from; pasted into a deck that lists no 9:16, it brought a layout the deck refuses. Now a pasted node keeps a layout only for a format the deck lays out anew, less its placement there, so it goes where it is pasted in each (`scaena_ops::inspect::copied_layouts`). A node held by a container is held by the container's copy in its formats' layouts too.

    - Done. Probing the editor's design tools found that a deck made or opened in the browser could never keep a history: the Versions tab said to run `scaena save --history`, which a person on the site cannot do. Now, where the bundle keeps none, the tab offers **Keep a history**. It saves the bundle where it is kept, or into the browser's storage, and that save begins the history (`Player.keepHistory`, `Session::save`): the deck as saved and the files it is drawn from are its first version, `user`'s `history begins`, as the CLI's is, and each save after records the edits since.
    - `scaena-history`'s `record`, handed an empty history, begins one with the first change it is given (`scaena_history::recorded`).
    - Tests: the WASM session's `a_page_begins_a_history_with_a_save` (no history unless asked; begun with the deck and its data file; the next save records on it), and `web/versions.mjs`, which presses Keep a history on the revenue example opened from its URL and finds one version, `history begins` by `user`.

- [x] 2.91 Room in the editor's module for the chart forms (1.29–1.31): it was 2,891 kB gzipped by Vite, 109 kB under SPEC §15's 3 MB, and each new chart form adds to it (the parent session, 2026-10-07).
    - Done. 2,891.27 kB to 2,811.36 kB gzipped by Vite (8,380.51 kB to 8,129.75 kB raw). `twiggy`, on the module built with its names, found what to cut.
        - Layout's crates build for size in the WASM profile: taffy, parley, harfrust, fontique, and the icu crates whose tables they read. They run as a deck is laid out, never as a frame samples it (SPEC §5): 68 KB of the cut, by `gzip -9`.
        - A map or a set built from an iterator goes through `scaena_core::sort::{map, try_map, set}`, which insert each item. `collect` into a `BTreeMap` sorts first, and compiled that sort for each element type and caller: 10 KB of the cut. The chart compiler's were left for the chart forms' own pass. After 1.31, it and three others build their sets so too, and `crates/scaena-wasm/tests/sorts.rs` finds a `collect` typed as a sorted map or set.
    - Timed in headless Chromium, with the CPU painter and no helper workers, on the trails deck:
        - Without a shader, a state paints in 7.69 ms on average. The two runs before the change read 8.12 and 7.96 ms.
        - The shader states paint as before: cover in 42.1 ms against 42.3, storm in 24.0 against 26.9, and next in 229.6 against 244.8.
        - B1 starts cold in 217 ms, against 218 before (the median of 9 runs).
    - Measured and not taken:
        - `scaena-core` at `z` is 252 KB smaller, but the shader states' CPU references paint 3.4–4× slower: cover in 143 ms, next in 1,012 ms.
        - `scaena-engine` at `z` is 156 KB smaller, but every frame without a shader paints 7% slower: 8.6 ms against 8.0, in two runs of each.
        - Neither is worth it while gate 2's first criterion, frames a second in a real machine's browsers, is still to be read.
        - `SemanticOp`'s deserializer, at 117 KB the largest function, is an internally tagged enum: serde buffers each op whichever deserializer reads it, so it stays as it is.
- [x] 2.92 Suggested layouts: the inspector draws the state shown in each layout it may take, judged by lint, best first, and one click gives it that layout (the parent session, 2026-10-07, at Jay's "ever forward").
    - Done. `scaena_ops::layouts` takes each layout `state_choices` offers the state, makes its `set_state`, written where the layout lives, lints the state in every format the deck lists, and draws it at rest. The layouts come fewest errors first, then fewest warnings; of those alike, the one the state takes now, then the theme's order. Each carries its patch and the states it changes.
    - A suggestion draws the state otherwise, or it is none. A layout drawn as one before it, with the same counts, is that one's `alike`: on trails' budget slide, `poster` and `full` set its note where `figure` does, so the slide is offered `figure` and `narrow-figure`. A state that places no node in a slot is offered none: a layout is its slots.
    - `scaena inspect --state S --layouts` lists them, and the MCP server's `deck_inspect` gives them with `layouts`, the assistant's too.
    - In the editor, with nothing selected, they stand above the state's fields (`Player.layoutSuggestions`), where there are two or more. Each is drawn at rest in the format shown, 72 pixels high, by the CPU painter in the worker, and says what lint finds there and which layouts draw it alike; the one the state takes now is pressed.
        - A pointer over one, or the focus on it, shows the state laid out in it on the canvas, nothing made (`Player.preview`), until the pointer or the focus leaves.
        - A click or Enter chooses it as the layout field does: one `set_state` by `user`, kept to the state with "layout only in", one step to undo.
        - They are judged again once edits pause, after the lint of every state, and when another state shows: typing never starts a round.
        - A round is the worker's longest task, so it judges a layout at a time (`Player.layoutsBegin`, `layoutsStep`) and answers what else the page asks between them; an edit meanwhile ends it. In headless Chromium on 4 cores, a round on the trails and revenue examples takes 80–430 ms, about 25–100 ms a layout. B1's edit round trip stays at a 28 ms median.
    - Tests: `crates/scaena-ops/tests/layouts.rs` (trails' budget slide offered figure, which poster and full draw alike, then narrow-figure in errors; storm a clean narrow-figure; the agenda one layout that every other draws alike; the change slide, its cards on the grid, none; `inspect` names the state), the WASM session's `a_states_layouts_come_judged_and_painted_best_first` (the CLI's list, painted, no two alike), and `web/suggest.mjs` (28 checks).
- [x] 2.93 What each of the assistant's edits changed, drawn: under each call that changed what a state draws, the state before and after, so the user sees each step the model took without looking for it (the parent session, 2026-10-07, at Jay's "ever forward").
    - Done. The page sends the strip's pictures' digests with the question (`Drawn`). As each edit is made, the worker draws each state whose drawing now differs at rest, as the strip draws it, before the assistant's next call can change it again, and sends the pictures with the edit. Each later edit is drawn against the one before.
    - The Assistant tab shows under the call each state before and after: the first four, how many more, and the states it took away. A state it added shows as new, and a click shows the state. Before the question goes, the strip draws what changed since it last did, so a user's own edit is not shown as the assistant's.

- [x] 2.94 A theme's colors from a photo: the photo's hues on the theme's own tones, each color text is set in kept where it reads (the parent session, 2026-10-07, at Jay's "ever forward").
    - Done. `scaena_core::palette` reads a photo in Oklab: its hues, best first, each scored by how much of the photo lies near it and how colorful it is there, and the hue all of it leans to. It gives a theme's colors those hues on the theme's own tones:
        - The accent takes the best hue, and the next chromatic color the best at least 60° from it. Each other chromatic color turns with the accent. Each keeps its lightness and chroma.
        - The neutrals take the photo's lean, a little of it.
        - A color text is set in moves the least step that reads on the surfaces at WCAG's ratio, as lint judges it.
        - The data palettes follow the theme's colors.
        - The theme's fonts, type, grid, layouts, and motion stay as they are.
    - The theme's chroma, not the photo's: both were tried on the repository's photos. The photo's chroma turned Daybreak's restrained teal into a loud orange-red, and its sequential palette jumped from near-white to salmon. The theme's chroma keeps each theme's style in the photo's hues.
    - It is a theme edit like any other (ADR-0016): `scaena theme --from-photo IMAGE`, `theme_edit`'s `photo` for the MCP server and the assistant, the Theme tab's From a photo, and a right click on a photo, Theme colors from this photo. Each is one `theme_edit` and one step to undo. A color within a quarter of a difference seen keeps how the theme writes it, so a photo taken again changes nothing.
    - On the trails example, the ridge photo turns the accent from #FF6A3D to #B97CFF (6.3:1 on the surfaces) and accent-2 from #FFC857 to #FFBFBA, and leans the neutrals violet: ink reads 15.7:1, muted 5.2:1. Lint finds nothing new.
    - The editor's module grows 11 KB gzipped. The palette reads only the theme's JSON it needs: the typed theme read from a `Value` compiled its deserializer a second time, 77 KB.
    - Tests: `scaena_core::palette`'s six (hues and lean, a gray photo, Dusk from a blue and orange photo, a light theme's accent darkened until it reads, a gray photo keeping the accents, and a photo taken again changing nothing), the CLI's `a_theme_takes_its_colors_from_a_photo_the_bundle_holds`, the MCP server's `a_theme_edit_takes_a_photos_colors`, the WASM session's `a_photo_gives_the_theme_its_colors`, and `web/theme-photo.mjs`.
- [x] 2.95 Lock a node, so the canvas can't move or delete it (the parent session, 2026-10-07, at Jay's "ever forward").
    - Done. A node's `locked: true` is for the editor and draws as nothing (deck format 0.17; SPEC §3.3). It is the node's own, in every state: a state's delta or `overrides` that sets it is E104, as `type` is.
    - The canvas passes over a node locked, and over what a locked container or group holds. A click, the marquee, ⌘A, Tab, hover, a dropped photo, and a double click all reach what is under it. This is Figma's lock, so a full-bleed photo under a slide's texts no longer catches every press. `Player.boxes` and `Player.hit` name the node whose lock holds each.
    - Selected in the layers, it is outlined dashed, with no handles. Its arrow keys, `[` and `]`, Enter, Delete, and ⌘X do nothing, and the status says why and that ⇧⌘L unlocks it.
    - The lock beside each layer's eye, ⇧⌘L, and the Lock or Unlock command lock and unlock. Each is one patch of the node's own `locked`, one step to undo. A right click where a locked node is offers to unlock it.
    - A patch, the CLI, the MCP server, and the assistant change a locked node as any other: a lock keeps the canvas's gestures off it. `scaena inspect --layers` says which are locked.
    - Tests: validation's E104 with a lock in a state and in the overrides, and a locked node itself clean; the CLI's `inspect_says_a_states_layers` with a node locked; and `web/lock.mjs` on the trails example's storm photo.
- [x] 2.96 Paste what another app copied: a picture as an image, a sheet's cells as a data source and the table they were, its figures as copied (the parent session, 2026-10-08, at Jay's "ever forward").
    - Done. ⌘V on the canvas took only a clip or words; a screenshot did nothing, and a sheet's cells came in as one line of text.
    - **A picture** joins the bundle and is inserted where the canvas was last pressed, as one dropped there is: a screenshot, a picture copied in a browser, or a file copied where files are kept, whose words only name it. One of a kind an image does not show, a GIF, a WebP, an AVIF, or a TIFF in Safari, joins as a PNG of it, dropped or pasted.
    - **A sheet's cells** (`scaena_core::data::cells`) are rows of cells split by tabs, as Numbers, Excel, and Google Sheets copy them. They become a data source, declared as `data_attach` declares a file and named for the first columns, and the table they were.
    - **Figures as copied.** A column of numbers one format writes in the deck's language (`$1,234.50`, `12.5%`, `(40)`, `1.234,50 €` in German) is written plainly in the CSV and declared `number`, and the table's column takes the format, so it prints each as copied and a chart can plot it. Any other column is text, even where its cells read as numbers, as `007` does.
    - **Text outranks a picture.** Excel copies a picture of the cells beside their text, and Keynote one of its words; the text pastes.
    - **Room for its columns.** A table, pasted or inserted, takes the grid's width where the half of the canvas Insert offers is too narrow for its columns in a format the deck lists. Before, the revenue example refused a five-column table: it fit 16:9, but not 9:16.
    - Tests: `scaena_core::data::cells`' six (a sheet's figures and their formats, what stays text, a German sheet, names from the first row, text that is no sheet, empty cells); the WASM session's `cells_pasted_from_a_sheet_become_a_source_and_a_table`; and `web/paste.mjs` on the revenue example.
- [x] 2.97 The light table: every slide in a grid, to move, copy, and take out across a large deck, each state showing what it showed (the parent session, 2026-10-08, at Jay's "ever forward").
    - Done. Slides shows the deck's slides in place of the preview, each at its last state, at rest, with its number, its id, its steps, and its findings (`web/src/slides.ts`). Selection is a file browser's: click, Shift+click, ⌘-click, the arrow keys, Space. Enter shows a slide in the canvas, and Escape closes the table.
    - **Every state shows what it showed.** States track from the state before them (SPEC §2.2), so moving a slide as `move_state` moves a state would change what the slide shows, and what the slide after it shows. Three new ops keep each state's look: `move_slide`, `duplicate_slide`, and `remove_slide` (SPEC §7.3). A state whose state before it changes has its delta written again, from the snapshots, to resolve as it did (`scaena_core::tracking::delta_to`, `keep_looks`). The deck is resolved after to prove it, and an order that cannot keep a look is refused with the state it would change.
    - **A copy is its own.** `duplicate_slide` makes the copy's first state absolute, setting every property of each node it shows, so an edit to either slide leaves the other; the copy shows the same nodes, so the slide morphs into it.
    - In the table: a drag, or Alt with an arrow, moves the slides selected; ⌘D copies them, the copies selected; Delete takes them out. Each is one patch, one step to undo. A slide's slot in the timeline now names its slide (`Player.timeline`), and the worker paints some states' thumbnails apart from the strip's.
    - Tests: `scaena_core::tracking`'s `a_delta_between_two_snapshots_resolves_as_the_second` and `states_moved_keep_their_looks`; the patch suite's `slides_move_copy_and_go_keeping_their_looks`; and `web/slides.mjs` on the revenue example, each state's reading compared before and after.
- [x] 2.98 Room under SPEC §15's 3.0 MB: core built for size, what is done pixel by pixel kept fast (the parent session, 2026-10-08, at Jay's "ever forward").
    - Done. Each shader kind's CPU reference and WGSL twin, and the JPEG decoder, move to a crate of their own, `scaena-pixels`, which core re-exports (`scaena_core::shader`, `scaena_core::jpeg`), so no caller changes. The `wasm` profile builds core at opt-level `z` and `scaena-pixels` at 3.
    - **The editor's module** is 2,595,394 bytes gzipped, from 2,850,052: 255 KB less, 405 KB under the budget where 2.97 left 150 KB. The player's, which a single file carries, is 2,126,786, from 2,179,408.
    - **What it costs.** In headless Chromium, on B1, compiling the source takes 9.6 ms, from 6.3, and linting the state edited 12.5 ms, from 8.7: a bit under half again, as typing goes. The editor's round trip is 35 ms, as gate 2 recorded it (`web/editor.mjs`), and linting every state 61 ms. Core at `s` saves 100 KB less for the same cost.
    - **What it keeps.** Shaders paint as fast: B3's states, 58–60 ms each, paint alike in both builds. Core alone at `s`, its shaders in it, paints B3 three times slower, 1,250 ms against 421. A 12-megapixel JPEG decodes in the same 200 ms, where core at `z` would take 35% longer. B1's paints, its frames, and its cold start are as they were.
    - Tests: every test moves with its code. `just wasm-smoke` and `just web-smoke` hold every torture state to the goldens, natively and in the browser, by WebGPU and the CPU painter; the `sorts` test walks `scaena-pixels` too; and CI's macOS runner takes a change to it as it takes one to the painters.

### Exit criteria (gate 2)
*(Evidence so far, and the runs that close it: `docs/gate-2.md`.)*
1. A deck authored in Phase 1 plays at 60 fps on WebGPU in Chrome and Safari 26+, and on the CPU fallback in Firefox at 60 fps where a cue draws no shader and 30 where it draws one (the bar Jay set on 2026-10-04: `docs/gate-2.md`).
2. Edit → lint → preview round trip < 200 ms for a 40-state deck.
3. Single-file export opens offline from a USB stick.
4. The assistant, with a user's own key, performs the Phase 1 agent-loop test inside the browser.

---

## Phase 3 — Mac client (≈ 6 weeks)

**Goal:** a native, document-based SwiftUI app on the same engine.

- [x] 3.1 `scaena-ffi` (C ABI via `cbindgen`): load bundle, states, timeline, `frame`, lint, patch, store ops; Swift package wrapper. *(Started 2026-10-08, on Jay's call, with gate 2's criterion 1 open.)*
    - **The session, shared.** The session the browser edits moved from `scaena-wasm` to `scaena-session`, unchanged, with its 77 tests and the random-edits fuzz (ADR-0021). `scaena-wasm` keeps `Player` and the WebGPU canvas, and the logic `Player` held moved into the session (`slots`, `theme_text`, `layouts_painted`, `keep_history`). `scaena-ffi` wraps the same session, so the Mac edits exactly as the browser does.
    - **The C ABI** (`crates/scaena-ffi/include/scaena.h`, written by cbindgen, held to the code by a test, `just bless`):
      - a bundle from its files, each by its path, or from a `.scaena` zip; Rust reads no filesystem, so a sandboxed app keeps its own access;
      - `scaena_call` answers the session's calls by the names a page uses: `states`, `timeline`, `compile`, `lint`, `inspect`, `layers`, `choices`, and the rest, the JSON `Player` gives;
      - `scaena_tool` runs any MCP operation on the bundle, as the browser's gestures and assistant do: a patch is `deck_patch`, by the author it names;
      - `scaena_frame` gives a display list and `scaena_pixels` the CPU painter's pixels;
      - `scaena_save` writes the bundle as `scaena save` does, fonts subset and the history recorded (`scaena_store::crdt::recorded`, which `scaena-history` now calls too), and `scaena_adopt` goes on from it.
    - **Failure is said.** A null, text that is not UTF-8, JSON that is not, a state the deck lacks, and a panic are each an error the call returns, never a crash.
    - **ScaenaKit** (`apps/mac/ScaenaKit`), the Swift package: `ScaenaSession` opens a folder, files, or a zip, and decodes each result (`Slot`, `Pixels`, `Saved`, `JSONValue`); a failure throws `ScaenaError`.
    - Tests: `scaena-ffi`'s `ffi` suite (the torture deck's states at rest digest as the goldens, a patch, a save with its history zipped and reopened, and every misuse an error); `header`; `just ffi`, a C program compiled against the header with every warning an error, on B1, in CI on Linux and macOS; and ScaenaKit's Swift Testing suite on CI's macOS runner.
- [ ] 3.2 Metal surface: `wgpu` surface from `CAMetalLayer`; `vello` painter in an `NSView`; 120 Hz on ProMotion. *(Built; the 120 Hz reading waits on a ProMotion Mac.)*
    - **The painter.** `scaena_paint::gpu::LayerPainter` paints on a `CAMetalLayer`: a `wgpu` surface on the layer (`SurfaceTargetUnsafe::CoreAnimationLayer`), on the GPU every layer in the process shares. That GPU is one adapter, one device, and one set of vello's pipelines and the shaders', made once (`scaena_paint::gpu::warm`): making them was most of a first frame's cost, 0.56 s of B1's 0.62 on CI's virtual Mac, and each surface with its own another 65–90 ms (gate 3). A frame renders into a storage texture of the layer's own as `GpuPainter`'s do, shaders first; a blit copies it onto the layer's next drawable, presented at the next refresh (FIFO). A layer with no drawable to give, hidden or resized meanwhile, skips the frame; an outdated one is configured again.
    - **The ABI.** `scaena_gpu_warm` (the GPU made, which the app calls as it starts, off its main thread), `scaena_surface_new` (a layer, its size in device pixels), `_resize`, `_paint` (a state at a time into its cue from a session, as the canvas shows it: 1 presented, 0 skipped, -1 an error said), `_pixels` (the last frame read back), `_adapter`, and `_free`. Each is in the one header on every platform; off the Mac, a surface is an error that says so, and the GPU crates are linked for Apple targets alone.
    - **The view.** ScaenaKit's `ScaenaSurface` wraps the calls. `ScaenaView` is an `NSView` whose backing layer is a `CAMetalLayer`, its drawable sized to the view in device pixels. A display link asks for 120 Hz (`CAFrameRateRange`, 30 to 120, 120 preferred), plays the state's cue from when it is set, paints it at rest once more, then idles until the state, the session, or the size changes. `ScaenaCanvas` is the view in SwiftUI.
    - Tests: the `ffi` suite's surface misuse (a null layer, and any layer off the Mac, an error); `cargo check` and clippy of `scaena-paint` and `scaena-ffi` for `aarch64-apple-darwin`, which type-checks the painter here; and ScaenaKit's `aSurfacePaintsOnMetalWhatTheCPUPainterPaints` on CI's macOS runner: B1's cover painted on Metal onto a layer no window shows, read back, and held to the CPU painter's pixels, a channel off by under a level on average.
    - **What waits.** The 120 Hz reading: a cue played in the app on a ProMotion Mac, its frame times read (3.3 gives the app).
- [ ] 3.3 Document-based app (`FileDocument`/`ReferenceFileDocument`) over bundles; autosave to CRDT; versions. *(Built; waits on a run on a Mac.)*
    - **The app.** `Scaena`, an executable in ScaenaKit's package, made into `Scaena.app` by `apps/mac/build-app.sh` (`just mac`): the release static library, the Swift app over it, and `apps/mac/Scaena/Info.plist`, which exports `com.scaena.deck`, a `.scaena` package the Finder shows as one file. Signed ad hoc. The `mac-app` workflow builds it on a Mac runner, when asked, as an artifact.
    - **The document.** `ScaenaDocument` is a `ReferenceFileDocument` over a bundle: a folder's files by their paths, or a `.scaena` zip, saved as the folder. A save is `scaena save`'s, fonts subset, and the session adopts it; each save records the edits since in the bundle's history, which the first save begins (SPEC §8). macOS keeps versions of a document saved in place. New is Dusk with one state, as the browser's New (`scaena_create`, from the themes that ship).
    - **The window.** The deck's states down the side, as the timeline plays them, each step named by its slide; the one chosen painted on Metal (3.2), its cue played as it is chosen. A slide's context menu duplicates or deletes it (`duplicate_slide`, `remove_slide`, PLAN 2.97), each a patch by the user and one step to undo and redo.
    - Tests: the `ffi` suite's `a_new_deck_starts_from_a_theme_that_ships`; and on CI's macOS runner, the app built from ScaenaKit's package.
- [ ] 3.4 Chrome: state list with thumbnails, timeline scrubber, inspector, source pane (same `.scn`), lint panel with one-click fixes. *(Built; waits on a run on a Mac.)*
    - **The editor, shared.** ScaenaKit's `DeckEditor` keeps a deck as the browser's editor does (PLAN 2.3): the source is what the deck is made from. A patch makes the deck's source again (`deck_patch`, then `source`), a source typed is compiled as it stands, and a finding's fix rewrites it (`fix`); each is compiled, becomes the deck where it validates, and lints the state shown at once, every state once edits stop. Each that changes the deck gives back the source it replaced, which `ScaenaDocument` registers as one step to undo; a source that does not compile is no deck to edit, and a gesture waits.
    - **The window.** The states down the side, each drawn small by the CPU painter, painted again only when its digest changes (as the browser's strip, PLAN 2.35). Under the canvas, the cue (PLAN 2.44's): Play, a ruler that scrubs it, and a bar for the transition and each motion, from `inspect`'s timeline. `ScaenaView` shows a `Playhead`, a time into the cue held or played on from by its own display link, and tells the ruler where it is some 30 times a second. A click selects the node the engine says draws there, passing over a locked one (`hit`), outlined through its `transform` (`boxes`): `Session::boxes_json` and `hits_json` moved out of `Player`, so the browser and the Mac read one answer. The inspector lists the state's layers and edits what the theme offers for the node selected, or for the state (`choices`, `stateChoices`), each choice one `choose` or `set_state`. The source pane is the deck's `.scn`: an `NSTextView` that keeps no undo of its own, compiled once typing pauses, the deck's source written back after any other edit. The source pane is chrome; the deck's text is the engine's, never TextKit's (SPEC §9.3). The findings panel lists what lint found, errors first, each going to its state and node, and Fix applies a fix lint has checked.
    - Tests: the `ffi` suite's `the_canvas_reads_what_stands_where_as_the_browser_does` (a box's middle hits its node; a lock is said) and `a_findings_fix_is_one_click_as_in_the_browser` (the title given the background's color, E110 or E111 with a fix, the fix compiled, the finding gone); ScaenaKit's `EditorTests` on CI's macOS runner: the inspector's, the layers', and the canvas's results typed; a choice linted, its fix in one click, undone and redone; a source typed that does not compile, then one that does; and a state's drawing kept until it changes.
    - **What waits.** A run of the app on a Mac.
- [ ] 3.5 Presentation mode: external display, presenter notes, remote (iOS later). *(Built; waits on a run on a Mac with a second display. The remote waits for iOS.)*
    - **The pace.** ScaenaKit's `Show` paces a deck as the browser's player does (PLAN 2.2, SPEC §2.4): going on plays the next state's cue, and going on during a cue finishes it; going back shows the state before at rest; a state that holds, short of the last, goes on by itself once its cue and its hold are over, and the last waits.
    - **The stage.** Play (⌥⌘P) fills the external display where there is one, and this one where there is not: a borderless window above the menu bar, the state's cue played on Metal from the state shown. The player's keys go on (→ ↓ Page Down Space Return, or a click) and back (← ↑ Page Up Delete), Home and End go to the first and the last state, and Escape ends.
    - **The presenter.** With an external display, the presenter's window takes this one: the state shown as the audience sees it, drawn on the stage's clock so one cue plays, not two; the next state at rest; the state's notes; where it is in the deck; and the time since the deck began. It takes the same keys, and closing it ends the show.
    - Tests: ScaenaKit's `aShowGoesOnAsTheBrowsersPlayerDoes` (a cue finished, the next played, the last waiting, back at rest, and holds).
    - **What waits.** A run on a Mac with a second display, and the remote, with iOS (SPEC §9.5, PLAN 4.10).
- [ ] 3.6 Keys in Keychain; BYOK assistant; Apple Foundation Models for on-device tasks. *(Built; waits on a run on a Mac: a question asked with a real key, and the on-device tasks on a Mac with Apple Intelligence.)*
    - **The conversation, in Rust** (ADR-0022). `scaena-chat` ports the browser's assistant (`web/src/assistant/`):
      - the conversation, in no provider's form;
      - each provider's request built from it and its answer read back: Anthropic's Messages API, its system prompt cached; OpenAI's Chat Completions, a frame sent as the user's image; Gemini's generateContent, its thought signatures sent back as they came;
      - the system prompt and the tools: the MCP tools' schemas less `bundle`, `out`, and `painter`, and `resource_read`;
      - the loop, as steps a client drives.

      It makes no HTTP call, reads no clock, and keeps no key.
    - **Held to the browser's.** `web/exchanges.mjs` runs the browser's adapters on one conversation (`tests/assistant/conversation.json`). It records, in `tests/assistant/exchanges.json`, what each adapter sends and reads, the models it lists, its failures, and the page's system prompt and bracketed line. scaena-chat's tests hold the Rust code to that record; CI's schema job and `just scripts` hold the TypeScript to it. A drift in either fails.
    - **The C ABI.**
      - `scaena_chat_new` begins a conversation with the model the user chose.
      - `scaena_chat_call` takes it a step at a time:
        - `ask`: the question, begun with what the window shows, the model told the deck as it is now;
        - `request`: the request for Swift to make, the key in its headers alone;
        - `answer`: the answer read;
        - `run`: a call run on the session by `agent:` and the model's name, as the browser runs it (`resource_read` from the resources; a frame as a PNG);
        - `next`: every call answered, those the user stopped as stopped;
        - `use`: another model, the conversation kept;
        - `forget`: the next question is the first.
      - `scaena_providers` lists the models a key can use.
      - The session's calls gain `carets` (a text's words as written) and `writeFiles` (files written back by an undo).
    - **On the Mac.**
      - ScaenaKit's `ScaenaChat` wraps the steps, and `Assistant` drives them:
        - each request is made with `URLSession`, under the app's sandbox and the system's proxies;
        - each call runs on the window's deck;
        - each edit is taken as the window's own (`DeckEditor.reread`) and is one step of its undo, the theme file `theme_edit` wrote written back with it.
      - `Keychain` keeps a key for each provider under the app's service, never in a bundle; Settings keeps the keys.
      - The window's Assistant (⌥⌘A):
        - lists the provider's models from its key;
        - begins each question with the state shown and the node selected;
        - shows what each call came to and the frame it drew;
        - Stop answers the calls not yet run as stopped.
    - **On this Mac.** With Apple's Foundation Models (macOS 26, Apple Intelligence):
      - Explain, beside a finding, says what it means for the deck;
      - the inspector drafts a state's notes from how it reads;
      - the inspector tightens a text's words.

      Each answer is offered to take as one patch (a `set_state` of `notes`, a `replace_text` of the whole text), or to leave. Nothing leaves the Mac; without the model, none is offered.
    - Tests:
      - scaena-chat's `exchanges`: the three adapters' requests and reads, models, and failures, and the page's prompt, against the record; a question run through two rounds; frames named past the newest two.
      - scaena-chat's unit tests: every MCP tool known; a summary for each kind of call.
      - The `ffi` suite's `the_assistant_asks_with_the_users_key_and_runs_each_call_on_the_bundle` (on B1: the request; three calls run, the frame a PNG; an edit by the agent; a model switched; a stop; a refusal) and `an_assistants_theme_edit_is_written_back_by_an_undo`.
      - ScaenaKit's `ChatTests` on CI's macOS runner: the conversation through the C ABI; a key's models; the loop, with a stand-in for the network, making the model's edit the window's own; and the Keychain, where the runner lets a test write it.
    - **What waits.** A question asked in the app with a real key, and the on-device tasks on a Mac with Apple Intelligence.
- [ ] 3.7 Direct manipulation v1: move/resize within template slots emits patches; off-template drags create explicit overrides (visibly flagged). *(Built; waits on a run on a Mac.)*
    - **As the browser's canvas** (PLAN 2.29–2.31, ADR-0013). A drag of a node, or a press on a node not selected, which selects it, moves it; a handle of the box selected, a corner or an edge's middle, resizes it.
      - How it snaps is the browser's `snapOf`, ported to ScaenaKit (`Targets.snap`):
        - on the theme's grid, by its tracks, or into the slot the box covers most;
        - in a stack, among its children; in a frame, where it is left;
        - Shift takes a node off the grid, as a `rect`, or puts one back on it.
      - As it moves, the engine draws the node moved, laying nothing out (`setMoving`). It says where the node lands, with the guides that box meets (`snap`), and the states the patch changes (`reach`). A resize that pauses shows its text reflowed (`preview`).
      - The drop is that one `place` patch by the user, written where the placement lives, or kept to the state shown with Option (`fork`): one step of the window's undo.
    - **Flagged.** A node placed off the theme's grid (a `rect` that no container holds) is outlined dashed in orange on the canvas, as lint's W301 flags it in the findings.
    - **The ABI.** `targets`, `snap`, `setMoving`, `preview`, and `reach` join the session's calls, each the answer `Player` gives a page. ScaenaKit wraps them (`Targets`, `Snapped`, `SnapMode`, `placements`), and `DeckEditor` draws the canvas again as a drag moves (`drawn`).
    - A turned or scaled node is moved in the browser's editor for now: the Mac says so.
    - Tests:
      - the `ffi` suite's `a_drag_on_the_canvas_ends_in_the_patch_the_browsers_does`, on B1: the title drawn moved and back; snapped into another slot; off the grid kept to the state; the patch previewed, reached, and made as previewed; and W301 found;
      - ScaenaKit's `PlacingTests` on CI's macOS runner: `snapOf`'s cases, and the drag through `DeckEditor`, undone.
    - **What waits.** A run of the app on a Mac.
- [ ] 3.8 Exports wired to Share sheet / Quick Look. *(Built; waits on a run on a Mac.)*
    - **The exports.** `scaena_export` makes `scaena export`'s bytes from the session:
      - `pdf`: a page for each slide at its last state, drawn as the browser's PDF module draws the pages the session lays out (`pdf_laid_out`, then `scaena_export::pdf::prepared`), byte for byte the CLI's;
      - `png`: a state at rest, painted by the CPU painter at the width asked.
    - **In the window.** The toolbar's Export menu:
      - shares the deck's PDF, or the state shown as a PNG, through the Share sheet (`NSSharingServicePicker`);
      - shows the PDF in Quick Look;
      - saves either where the user says.

      Each is written when it is asked for, named for the deck.
    - Tests: the `ffi` suite's `an_export_is_the_bytes_scaena_export_writes` (B1's PDF is the CLI's, byte for byte; a PNG 640 wide; misuse said); ScaenaKit's `theDeckExportsAsTheCLIWritesIt` on CI's macOS runner.
    - **What waits.** A run on a Mac. A Quick Look preview of a `.scaena` bundle in the Finder needs an app extension, which an Xcode project builds and the Swift package cannot: it waits for the app's Xcode project.
- [ ] 3.9 Typing on the canvas: a double click types in a text where it stands, as the browser's canvas does (PLAN 2.32). Keys and an input method's composition come through `NSTextInputClient`, which lays nothing out; the caret, the selection, and up, down, home, and end come from the engine's carets. Each change is a `replace_text` patch written where the text lives, a burst of typing one step of undo, and ⌘B and ⌘I give the characters selected a look (PLAN 2.38, 2.40). *(Added on 2026-10-08 on Jay's call, with 3.10–3.17: the Mac edits as the browser's editor does. Built; waits on a run on a Mac.)*
    - **As the browser's canvas** (PLAN 2.32). A double click on a text puts a caret in it where the engine says the character there stands, kept to the state shown with Option (`fork`). A press in the text, or within 4 points of its box, puts the caret there; a second click selects the word there, a third its paragraph, and a drag selects. A press outside it, another node selected, another state shown, focus gone to the source or a field, Tab, or Escape stops typing; the node stays selected.
    - **Keys through the text input system.** `CanvasKeys` (ScaenaKit), an `NSView` under the canvas's presses, takes the keyboard while a text is typed in and answers `NSTextInputClient` from the engine's carets (`Carets`, the browser's `carets` call): what is typed, the commands the key bindings make of keys (deletes by character, word, and line; moves by character, word, line, line's end, and the text's; each with Shift), and the Edit menu's Copy, Cut, and Paste as plain text. It lays nothing out and draws nothing; `Carets` reads where a caret stands, where a line goes up or down, and what a selection covers, as `web/src/typing.ts` reads them, in UTF-16, as the input system counts.
    - **An input method's composition** is typed in place, so the engine sets it as it will read, its clause underlined: committed, it stays where it is; an accent chosen for the letter before replaces it.
    - **The patches.** Each change is one `replace_text` by the user, its offsets in characters, written where the text lives, validated but not linted (`typed`, as `Player.typed`): lint runs once typing stops. A burst of typing, its keys less than a second apart with nothing else edited between, is one step of the window's undo. ⌘B and ⌘I give the characters selected the look the engine reads for them (`bolding`, `italicizing`), one `style_text`, a step of its own.
    - **The ABI.** `typed`, `bolding`, and `italicizing` join the session's calls, each the answer `Player` gives a page.
    - Tests:
      - the `ffi` suite's `text_typed_in_place_is_the_patch_the_browsers_typing_makes`, on B1: words typed after the title, the same words over themselves changing nothing, ⌘B's and ⌘I's looks given, and misuse said;
      - ScaenaKit's `TypingTests` on CI's macOS runner: the carets read, a press, a word, and a paragraph on B1's subtitle, up and down its two lines, and keys, a composition, an accent, and ⌘B through `CanvasKeys`, the burst undone in one step;
      - `chrome.rs` holds the new Swift to gate 3's third criterion: nothing in it lays text out, measures it, or draws it.
    - **What waits.** A run on a Mac, with an input method that composes (Japanese, say), whose candidate window stands at the caret.
- [ ] 3.10 A text's characters in the inspector, its links, and its lists: a role or a color for the characters selected (`style_text`, PLAN 2.38), ⌘K links them (PLAN 2.70), and a list's keys (PLAN 2.69). *(Built; waits on a run on a Mac.)*
    - **The characters' look.** With characters selected in a text typed in, the inspector offers what the engine offers for them (`characterChoices`, `scaena_core::choices::characters`): a run's role, emphasis, family, weight, italic, and color, each the first character's. Each choice is one `style_text` by the user, written where the text lives; Theme's takes the run's own away, so the text's look shows there.
    - **⌘K** asks where the characters selected link to: a web address (`https://`, `http://`, `mailto:`) or a state's id, `#` before it or not. Left empty, it takes their link away. One `style_text` of `link`. The question is a field over the canvas; focus there, or in the inspector's fields, leaves typing as it is, as the browser's does. In Play, a click on a link at rest follows it (`linkAt`, `Show.go`): a state plays its cue, and a web address opens in the browser.
    - **A list's keys** (ADR-0018), as a word processor's:
      - ⌘⇧8 and ⌘⇧7 bullet or number the paragraphs the selection touches, by the keys' places; on items of that kind already, they take them out of the list;
      - Tab and Shift+Tab move the items a level in or out; outside a list, Tab stops typing;
      - Return makes an item like the one it leaves (`replace_text` keeps `list` in step), and Return in an empty item ends the list there.

      Each is one `list` patch, one step to undo.
    - **The ABI.** `characterChoices` and `linkAt` join the session's calls.
    - Tests:
      - the `ffi` suite's `a_texts_characters_take_a_look_a_link_and_a_list` on B1: what is offered for the title's characters, a link made and found where it is drawn, and the subtitle bulleted;
      - ScaenaKit's `TypingTests`: a list's keys through `CanvasKeys` on B1's subtitle, five steps to undo; a link asked, made, refused, taken away, and dismissed; and a role chosen for characters;
      - `ShowTests`: a link goes to a state, its cue played.
    - **What waits.** A run on a Mac.
- [ ] 3.11 Insert, delete, duplicate, and lock: the Insert menu from what the theme and the bundle offer, landing in the room nearest the pointer (PLAN 2.34, 2.79); Delete, Shift+Delete, and ⌘D; ⇧⌘L locks a node (PLAN 2.95). *(Built; waits on a run on a Mac.)*
    - **The Node menu** acts on the window in front, through a focused scene value:
      - Insert lists, by kind, what the theme and the bundle offer (`inserts`). What is chosen lands where the pointer last pressed on the canvas, or in the room nearest it where content would overlap (`inserting`). It enters in the state shown, selected.
      - Duplicate (⌘D) puts a copy beside the node selected (`duplicating`).
      - Delete takes the node out of the state shown and those after, and out of the deck where no state shows it after; Delete from the Deck takes it out everywhere (`deleting`). A locked node is not taken.
      - Lock (⇧⌘L) sets the node's own `locked`, in every state, or takes it away. So does the lock beside each layer in the inspector.

      Each is one patch, one step to undo. While a text is typed in, the menu waits, and the keys are the text's.
    - **The canvas's keys.** A press on the canvas gives it the keyboard, as the browser's canvas takes the focus. `CanvasKeys` takes the keys: the text's while one is typed in (PLAN 3.9); otherwise Delete and Shift+Delete take the node selected away, and Escape selects what holds it.
    - **The ABI.** `inserting`, `duplicating`, and `deleting` join the session's calls.
    - Tests:
      - the `ffi` suite's `a_node_is_inserted_copied_and_taken_away_as_the_browser_does` on B1: a rect offered, inserted, made, and copied; deleted from the deck, a headline the next states show deleted from its state on, and with Shift from the deck; misuse said;
      - ScaenaKit's `NodesTests`: Lock's patch, escaped as JSON pointers, as the browser's; a rect inserted, copied, locked, unlocked, and deleted through `DeckEditor`; and the canvas's keys, the canvas's with no text typed in and the text's while one is.
    - **What waits.** A run on a Mac.
- [ ] 3.12 The clipboard: ⌘C, ⌘X, and ⌘V copy, cut, and paste a node as a clip (PLAN 2.37); a picture or a sheet's cells another app copied paste as what they are (PLAN 2.96); ⌥⌘C and ⌥⌘V copy and paste a look (PLAN 2.58). *(Built; waits on a run on a Mac.)*
    - **Nodes.** With the canvas focused and no text typed in, the Edit menu's Copy and Cut put the node selected on the pasteboard as a clip (`copying`). The clip goes under its own type, and beside it as its text, which the browser's editor pastes too; Cut then deletes the node from the state shown on. Paste puts a clip's nodes where the pointer last pressed, as Insert places a node, under ids new to the deck (`pasting`), in this deck or another. The status says what the theme lacked, which was taken out.
    - **From another app** (`Pasteboard.read`, as the browser reads its clipboard):
      - a picture, a screenshot or a file copied in the Finder, with the words beside it only naming it, is kept by its content (`scaena_drop`) and inserted as an image; a picture of a kind an image does not show comes in as a PNG of it;
      - a data file comes in as a source and a chart of it;
      - a sheet's cells, which outrank the picture of them that comes along, come in as a source typed as they read (`cells`, `attaching`) and the table they were, each column printing its figures as they were copied (`inserting`'s `with`);
      - other words come in as a text in the theme's body role.
    - **A look.** ⌥⌘C copies the look of the node selected (`look`), and ⌥⌘V puts it on the node selected (`putting`): one patch of `choose`s, each written where that node's own value lives.

      Each paste is a step to undo, and a file's source declared is one before it.
    - **The ABI.** `copying`, `pasting`, `look`, `putting`, `cells`, and `attaching` join the session's calls, and `inserting` takes `with`. `scaena_drop` hands over a file dropped or pasted where the browser's canvas keeps one.
    - Tests:
      - the `ffi` suite's `the_clipboard_copies_and_pastes_as_the_browsers_does` on B1:
        - the title copied, and pasted in another state;
        - words pasted as a text;
        - a look put on the subtitle;
        - a sheet's cells read, their file dropped and attached;
        - a picture dropped, and offered to insert;
        - misuse said;
      - ScaenaKit's `ClipboardTests`:
        - the pasteboard read as the browser's clipboard is: a clip beside its text, a picture, words outranking a picture of them, and a TIFF as a PNG;
        - through `DeckEditor`, a clip, words, a look, a sheet's cells with their table, and a picture, each made.
    - **What waits.** A run on a Mac, copying from Numbers, Preview, and the Finder.
- [ ] 3.13 Several selected: Shift+click and a marquee, moved together, aligned, spread, and ordered; ⌘G and ⌘⇧G group and ungroup (PLAN 2.42, 2.43). *(Built; waits on a run on a Mac.)*
    - **Selecting several.** Shift and a click add a child of the same container to what is selected, or take one out. A drag from where nothing draws is a marquee: it selects what lies wholly inside it, children of the canvas a pointer reaches; with Shift, it adds them.
    - **Moving them.** A drag of one of them moves them all, as the browser's canvas moves several (`together`): the engine draws them moved (`setMoving` takes several), the first snapped as it would be alone and the rest as far as it went, with the guides the box around them meets. The drop is one patch, kept to the state with Option.
    - **Acting on them.** Delete, Duplicate, Copy, Cut, Lock, and Paste Look act on every node selected, each one patch; a paste of several selects them all.
    - **The inspector** offers what they all share (`Field.shared`), each choice a `choose` for each node in one patch, and buttons that align them on an edge or a middle, spread them across or down, put them in front or behind (`arranging`), and group them.
    - **The Node menu** groups them where they stand (⌘G, `grouping`), takes a group apart (⌘⇧G, an `ungroup`), and orders them among what their container paints (⌘], ⌘[, with Option to the front and the back).
    - **The ABI.** `together`, `arranging`, and `grouping` join the session's calls.
    - Tests:
      - the `ffi` suite's `several_nodes_move_arrange_and_group_together` on B1: three nodes moved together, aligned, spread, and ordered, grouped and taken apart, and a way that is none said;
      - ScaenaKit's `SeveralTests`: what several share, and the same through `DeckEditor`.
    - **What waits.** A run on a Mac.
- [ ] 3.14 States and slides: the state list adds a step or a slide, moves, renames, and removes states (PLAN 2.35); the light table (PLAN 2.97); rehearse (PLAN 2.63); the cue's bars dragged (PLAN 2.44). *(Built; waits on a run on a Mac.)*
    - **The state list** adds a step after a state or a slide after its slide, from a row's menu or the list's Add (`addingState`), the new state shown. It renames a state (`rename_state`, its links renamed with it), moves it by a drag or a step up or down (`move_state`), and removes it (`remove_state`), the state after it shown. Each is one patch, one step to undo.
    - **The light table.** Slides (⌥⌘L) shows every slide in place of the canvas, each drawn at its last state at rest. A click, Shift+click, and ⌘-click select slides, and ← → Home End move the selection. The slides selected, dragged onto another, go before it or after it; ⌥← and ⌥→ move them a place; ⌘D copies them, each just after itself; Delete takes them out. Each is one patch of `move_slide`, `duplicate_slide`, or `remove_slide`, and every other state shows what it showed. Return, or a double click, shows a slide's first state on the canvas.
    - **Rehearse** (⌥⌘R) plays the deck in the window as presented, from its first state, and keeps the time each state is shown (ScaenaKit's `Rehearsal`, which is told the time and keeps no clock). → ↓ Page Down Space Return or a click go on, ← ↑ Page Up Delete go back, and Escape stops, as does going on from the last state. At the end, what each state took: Keep makes each state reached hold that long, less its cue, to a tenth of a second, one `set_state` of `hold` each, in one patch.
    - **The cue's bars** (`Cue.bars`, as the browser's): a motion's bar dragged moves its start, and its end its duration, each a `time_motion` to a whole ten of ms, written where the motion is; the transition's end is a `set_state` of its duration; a spring's end does not move. A press on a motion's bar selects its node. Add Motion offers the theme's presets on the node selected, as it enters or for emphasis, and on a node that leaves, as it leaves (`motionOffers`, `apply_preset`, an exit written in the state the node leaves).
    - **The ABI.** `addingState` joins the session's calls.
    - Tests:
      - the `ffi` suite's `states_and_slides_are_added_moved_and_timed_as_the_browsers_are` on B1 and the torture deck: a step and a slide added, a state renamed, moved, and removed, a slide moved, copied, and taken out, a hold kept, a motion timed, the transition's end moved, and a preset added;
      - ScaenaKit's `StatesTests`: the same through `DeckEditor`, a rehearsal's times and the holds they keep, and an exit offered and written in the state before.
    - **What waits.** A run on a Mac.
- [ ] 3.15 The panels: the theme (its colors, type, and spacing; another theme; one from a photo, PLAN 2.39, 2.61, 2.94), the data (a source as a table, edited; quotes, PLAN 2.55, 2.72), the files (PLAN 2.59), and the versions the history keeps (PLAN 2.60). *(Built; waits on a run on a Mac.)*
    - **Where they are.** The inspector's column holds the panels, each chosen by a button above it: Node (the inspector), Theme, Data, Files, and Versions, as the browser's tabs at the right.
    - **The theme.** Another theme, one the bundle holds or one that ships with its fonts (`retheme`), refused with why where the deck would not validate in it; each color by a swatch, which picks one, and its value as the theme writes it; each type role's size, weight, leading, and tracking; the grid's gutter and baseline, and the space unit; and a photo's colors (PLAN 2.94). Each is one `theme_edit` by the user (`themeEdit`), one step to undo, which writes the theme file back.
    - **The data.** A data source as a table: a cell set when typed, a row added, or one taken away, each value read by its column's type, all or none (`dataEdit`); a cell its column does not read is marked, and says why. An edit writes the source's file, which the window's undo puts back by the session's own (`dataUndo`), or the deck's rows written inline, which the source's undo takes back. A quote of the data set again goes back with it.
    - **The files.** The bundle's images, fonts, and data, each with what names it and the nodes drawn from it (`bundleFiles`); one nothing names is taken out (`removeFile`), one step to undo, which puts it back.
    - **The versions** the bundle's history keeps, each by its author and time, read from the history natively, as `scaena history` reads it (`versions`). One chosen is shown read only, a state of it drawn (`viewVersion`, and `scaena_export`'s `version`), and compared with the deck now (`compareVersions`). Restore makes it the deck again with its data files and its theme (`restoreVersion`), one step to undo, which writes them back. Where the bundle keeps no history, Keep a History begins one with the next save (PLAN 2.87).
    - **The ABI.** `shippedThemes`, `retheme`, `themeEdit`, `bundleFiles`, `removeFile`, `dataSources`, `dataSheet`, `dataEdit`, `dataUndo`, `versions`, `viewVersion`, `compareVersions`, and `restoreVersion` join the session's calls.
    - Tests:
      - the `ffi` suite's `the_theme_is_edited_and_another_taken_as_the_browsers_theme_tab_does` on the revenue example, `data_is_edited_and_files_taken_out_as_the_browsers_panels_do` on the torture deck, and `versions_are_listed_shown_compared_and_restored_from_the_history` on B1 saved twice;
      - ScaenaKit's `PanelsTests`: the same, a deck from Dusk re-themed and B1 refused, rows added and taken away, and the theme written back as an undo writes it.
    - **What waits.** A run on a Mac.
- [ ] 3.16 Handles and views: the rotate handle (PLAN 2.51), points and corners (PLAN 2.68), an image's crop and focal point (PLAN 2.45, 2.74), the grid and its guides (PLAN 2.57), zoom and pan (PLAN 2.46), the formats side by side (PLAN 2.62, 2.85), and find and replace (PLAN 2.47). *(Built; waits on a run on a Mac.)*
    - **The rotate handle** stands above the box of the node selected, where its top edge is drawn. A drag turns the node about its anchor as the pointer goes round, past a half turn and on, in whole degrees or with Shift in fifteens (ScaenaKit's `Turn`, the browser's turn, from the node's `transform` as the state resolves it, `turned`); what holds it mirrored turns it the other way. Let go, one `choose` of `transform/rotate`.
    - **Points and corners** (`outline`). A line's, an arrow's, or a polygon's points have handles: one dragged goes to the point of the box under the pointer, a hundredth at a time, kept to the box; a press at the middle of an edge adds one there; a point clicked is picked, and Delete takes it away, never below the two or three its kind keeps. A rect's corner handle, in from its corner as far as its radius, rounds the corners to the theme's radius step its drag reaches along the diagonal. Each is one `choose` of `points` or `radius`.
    - **An image's crop and focal point** (`framing`). A bar inside each side of the part that shows crops the image from that side, a twentieth of it kept at least, the whole image outlined as it goes and what the crop cuts away shaded; the ring at the focal point moves it within the box. Each is one `choose` of `crop` (none where it keeps the whole image) or `focal`, a thousandth at a time. `focalAt` reads the point of the image under a press, as Pick does.
    - **Turned and scaled nodes.** A drag moves a node as what holds it lays it out, read back through whatever turns or scales that, and a handle resizes it along its own sides (`across`), as the browser's canvas does (PLAN 2.51). Its handles stand where they are drawn.
    - **The grid.** ⌘', or the toolbar's Grid, draws the theme's grid over the canvas (`grid`): its columns and rows as bands, the margins dashed, and the baseline grid's lines.
    - Each handle is kept to the state shown with Option, and is one step to undo.
    - **Zoom and pan** (ScaenaKit's `Zoom`, the browser's preview): ⌘= and ⌘− a step closer or farther, from the whole canvas to eight times as close, and ⌘0 the whole canvas again; a pinch, or the wheel with ⌘, zooms about the pointer, and the wheel pans what is zoomed in, kept on the canvas. The Metal surface paints through the part shown (`setView`, `DisplayList::viewed`), and the canvas reads every press, and draws its outlines and handles, through it, as does the caret an input method asks for.
    - **The formats side by side.** Formats shows the state shown under the canvas in the deck's own canvas and each format it lists, each drawn at rest by the CPU painter a format at a time (`scaena_pixels_in`), counting the findings about the state that hold there (each finding's `formats`). A click shows the canvas in that format (`setFormat`, `DeckEditor.show(format:)`): its boxes, targets, grid, and what lint says hold there, and a drag of a node with a layout of its own there moves it there alone (PLAN 2.85). The Node menu's Place Anew in This Format gives the node selected one, where it stands (`placingAnew`, a `place` with `anew`).
    - **Find and replace** (⌘F on the canvas, ⇧⌘F, or the toolbar's Find): a bar over the canvas finds what is typed in the deck's words in every state (`find`), Match Case and Whole Words as asked. Return and Shift+Return go to the next match and the one before (`Matches`), each shown in its state with its node selected; Replace replaces the match shown (`replacing` with `one`) and shows the next, and Replace All every match in one patch, each one step to undo. Escape closes it.
    - **The ABI.** `outline`, `framing`, `focalAt`, `grid`, `setView`, `find`, and `replacing` join the session's calls, and `scaena_pixels_in` paints a state in one of the deck's formats, or on its own canvas, a height high.
    - Tests:
      - the `ffi` suite's `handles_and_views_read_as_the_browsers_canvas_reads_them` on the torture deck and B1: a rect's radius and steps, a polygon's points, an image's crop and the point under a press, the grid's twelve columns, a view refused without a size, a format's pixels, and find and replace, each match or one;
      - ScaenaKit's `HandlesTests`: the corner's step reached and made, a point moved, added, and kept from going below three, a turn past a half turn and in fifteens, then made; a crop from each side kept to a twentieth, the focal point moved, the whole image no crop; and the grid, the view, a format, and find on B1;
      - ScaenaKit's `ViewsTests`: zoom by steps, about a point, kept on the canvas and to eight times, and the whole canvas again; the canvas in 9:16 and back, a format the deck does not list refused, a node placed anew there once, and findings saying the formats they hold in; find's matches stepped both ways and round, and the Edit menu's Find taken by the canvas.
    - **What waits.** A run on a Mac.
- [ ] 3.17 By keys alone and for VoiceOver: the canvas by keys (PLAN 2.75, 2.89), each node an accessibility element that reads as the reader hears it (PLAN 2.8, 2.56). *(Built; waits on a run on a Mac.)*
    - **The canvas by keys** (as the browser's, PLAN 2.75), with the canvas focused and no text typed in: CanvasKeys hands each key to the canvas before the input system (`pressed`). Tab and Shift+Tab select the next node, or the one before, in reading order among those beside it (`readingOrder`: as they are painted, a container where the first it holds is, what is locked passed over); past either end nothing is selected and the keyboard goes on. Return goes into a container or a group, onto a shape's or an image's handles (`KeyedHandle`), which Tab then steps through, or, on a text, types in it; Escape comes back out, a step at a time.
    - **The arrows** step the node selected as its drag would (`Targets.stepped`, the browser's): a move to the next track's start, with Shift its end to the next track's end, off the grid a unit, in a stack past the next child; several move together, and Option keeps each to the state shown. On a handle, an arrow moves it a hundredth, with Shift a tenth, a corner a radius step, a crop side across itself (`KeyedHandle.nudged`); + adds a point after the one the keys are on, and Delete takes it away. `[` and `]` turn the node 15° back or on, with Shift 1°. ⌘A selects all beside it. Each is one patch, one step to undo.
    - **A selection by keys** (PLAN 2.89): Space starts it on the node selected, Tab then keys the next beside it, Space puts it in or takes it out, and Escape stops, what is selected staying.
    - **VoiceOver.** Each node a reader hears is an element of the canvas where it is drawn, in reading order, saying what it is as the reading HTML says it (`reads`, `scaena_core::reading::parts`): a heading at its level, words, a figure by its alt text, a table by its alt text or its cells; selected, it says so, and its action selects it.
    - **The ABI.** `reads {state}` joins the session's calls.
    - Tests:
      - the `ffi` suite's `a_state_reads_node_by_node_as_a_screen_reader_hears_it` on B1 and the torture deck;
      - ScaenaKit's `KeysTests`: B1's cover read as two headings; the reading order of a state with containers; a node stepped a column, its end a column, and nowhere past the edge, then made; a child stepped past the next in its stack; a selection built by Space, a node put in after the rest, one taken out, and one in another container starting it anew (`toggled`); each handle moved by keys as its drag would; and a key read by the canvas before the input system.
    - **What waits.** A run on a Mac.
- [ ] 3.18 The light window: the Mac's window as a presentation app's, in a person's words. *(Added on 2026-10-09 on Jay's call, with 3.19–3.20: "lightweight and easy, not hardcore and academic". The iPad compiles the same sources and takes it too. Built; waits on a run on a Mac.)*
    - **Words.** Nothing the deck's format names reaches the window as the format names it (`Words`, `ThemeLook`). Slides are numbered, and a step is set in under its slide; an object is its first words, else its kind; a property is what a presentation app calls it (Text Style, Font, Fill, Build In, Advance); a value is said (a color by what it is for, with its swatch; a font by its family; a duration with its seconds; a weight by its name; a hold as On Click or After 5.5 s); the theme's value is Default. No id, property path, lint code, source line, or millisecond is shown, and what an edit did is said without one.
    - **The toolbar**, as Keynote's: View (the light table; the timeline, other sizes, grid, issues, and source) and Zoom; Add Slide (a step from its menu) and Play; Text, Shape, Image, Chart, and Table, each a click that inserts the first the deck offers of its kind and a menu of the rest (`InsertMenu`); Share and the assistant; and Format, Animate, and Document, the inspector's tabs. The timeline, the other sizes, the issues, and the source start off. The slide sits on the gray a slide sits on, lifted off it by a shadow.
    - **The inspector** by tab: Format shows the object selected in sections by what it is (its look; Fitting for a text; Arrange, with its opacity, its rotation, and its position and size typed, which is the `place` a drag there makes; its Description; the rest under More), or, with nothing selected, the slide's layout and presenter notes and the objects on it; Animate shows an object's builds, or the slide's transition and how it advances; Document holds the theme, the data, the media, and the history (PLAN 3.15). Each choice is the patch it was.
    - **The menus**: Insert, by kind; Arrange (front and back, group, lock); in Edit, Duplicate, Delete, and Copy and Paste Style; in View, the light table, each part of the window, and the inspector's tabs; and Play, with Play Slideshow and Rehearse Slideshow. Each toolbar item is a menu command, with its key there.
    - Tests: ScaenaKit's typing tests hold its status to the new words; the iPad's UI tests find the canvas, the slides, and the text typed in by their accessibility identifiers, which VoiceOver does not read, and the light table from the toolbar's View menu.
    - **What waits.** A run on a Mac.
- [ ] 3.19 Move anything anywhere (ADR-0024, proposed): a drag lands where it is let go. *(Built; waits on a run on a Mac.)*
    - **Free, with magnets.** A move or a resize of a node the theme's grid holds is `free`. Within 8 points on screen, the grid's tracks, the canvas's edges and middle, and every other object's edges and middles draw it in, with guides where they meet; ⌘ holds them off. On the tracks all round it takes their cells, so a deck lined up with the grid keeps a placement the theme moves in every format (`Session::guided_freely`, the C ABI's `snap` with `grid: true`).
    - **Shift** snaps as before: into a slot, or onto the grid's cells, a `rect` back onto them (`Targets.dragged`). The arrow keys step by tracks, as before. What a container holds goes as its container places it.
    - **Nothing marks it.** No orange outline; W301 and W302 are not among the issues the editors show, and stay lint rules for the CLI, the MCP server, the assistant, and the browser.
    - Tests: the session's `a_drag_lands_where_it_is_let_go_the_grid_drawing_it_in` (the torture deck's card let go a column over and 3 units off takes the cells there; with no reach, a `rect` where it was let go; a stack's child goes nowhere free); ScaenaKit's `aDragLandsWhereItIsLetGo` (the policy, and B1's title onto the grid's first four columns and two rows, or off them by a `rect`).
- [ ] 3.20 Issues where they are: what a person can put right on a slide, marked on its object in a sentence, with its fix. *(Built; waits on a run on a Mac.)*
    - **What is marked** (`Issues`): text that does not fit (E100, W202, W203) or cannot be read against what is behind it (E110, E111); objects that overlap (E101); characters its font lacks (E120), or an italic it lacks (W231); a chart's labels that overlap (W310), an effect behind it (W311), text too small to read (W312), or the chart squashed (W313); a picture with no description (W410); a figure its data no longer gives (W427). Each in the format shown, at its object's top right: red for an error, orange for a warning.
    - **A click** on the mark says what is wrong in a sentence ("It's hard to read against what's behind it."), with the fix where lint has one (Use a Color That Reads, Shrink to Fit, Update the Figure), one step to undo. Nothing is marked while a drag, a handle, or typing goes on.
    - **The rest** (the deck's structure, its story, its motion, its placement) stays in View › Issues, which lists everything but W301 and W302 (ADR-0024), and with the CLI, the MCP server, and the browser.
- [ ] 3.21 A text inserted is typed in, its words selected, so what is typed next takes their place, as a presentation app's new text box is. *(Built; waits on a run on a Mac.)*
    - **Where.** The toolbar's Text and the Insert menu insert where the pointer last pressed, as before (PLAN 3.11). A text enters typed in (`Typing.enter`), every character selected (`selectAll`); anything else enters selected, as before. Escape stops typing and leaves the text selected.
    - Tests: the iPad's UI test `testATextInsertedIsTypedIn`, B1's toolbar's Text tapped and the canvas typing in what it inserted.
    - **Seen on CI's Mac.** `apps/mac/window.sh` walks the window after opening trails: the window fitted to the screen, a click on the slide, Tab, Insert › Text, and words typed in its place, each screen kept and pushed beside the iPad's (`scripts/shots.sh`), and where the window stands said. The window opens within the screen: the slide fits the room the window gives it and does not size the window (`fitted`).
- [ ] 3.22 Drop a file on the slide, as the browser's canvas takes one (PLAN 2.45, 2.76): a picture from the Finder, Files, Photos, or a browser, or a CSV or JSON file. *(Built; waits on a run on a Mac.)*
    - **Where it lands.** A picture let go on an image takes its place, one `choose` of `src`; anywhere else it is inserted there, as Insert inserts one. A data file joins the deck as a source, declared as `data_attach` declares it, with a chart of it there. A PNG or a JPEG goes in as it is, any other picture as a PNG of it; the slide is outlined while a drag that holds one is over it. Each is one step to undo.
    - **How** (`Pasteboard.dropped`): each thing dropped gives its file as the Finder or Files dragged it, or a copy of a picture's data, by its name.
    - Tests: ScaenaKit's `aDroppedFileIsReadAsThePasteboardsIs`: a PNG and a CSV by their names, a TIFF's data as a PNG of it, and words not at all.
